$ . Q . 2110 . - In pursuance of clause ( a ) of Section 2 of the Diplomatic and 
Consular Officers ( Oaths and Fees ) Act , 1948 (41 of 1948 ) , the Central Govern

MINISTRY OF FINANCE 


( Department of Expenditure ) 


New Delhi, the 19th August 1960 


$ . O . 2121 . - In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to article 
309 of the Constitution and of all other powers enabling him in that behalf, the 
President hercby directs that the following further amendments shall be made 
in the Civil Service Regulations, namely : 
1. These Regulations may be called the Civil Service (Amendment) 

Regulations, 1960 . 
2. In the Civil Service Regulations 
In the list of services and appointments below article 349 - A ( 1 ) , for the 
existing ontry " Civilian Officer Supervisors at Army Headquarters and Air Head 
quarters" , the following entry shall be substituted , namely : 
" Civilian Staff Officers, Grade I, Civilian Staff Officers , Grade II and . 

Civilian Officer Supervisors at Army Headquarters, Naval Flead 

quarters , Air Headquarters and Inter- service Organisations, 
2 . The amendment shall be deemed to have taken cffect on the 1st August. 
1951. 

(No. F . 24 (47 ) -EV /60 . 1 


S . O . 2122 . - In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to article 309 
of the Constitution , the President hereby makes the following further amendment 
in the Rules regulating the Workmen s Contributory Provident Fund as instituted 
with the Government of India late Finance Department Resolution No. F . 33 ( 3 ) - RII / 
44 dated the 16th April , 1945, and as amended from time to time, namely : 


In the sald Resolution , in paragraph 2, for clause ( 1) the following shall be 
cubstituted , namely : 


" ( 1 ). Every workman who has had at least one year s continuous service on 
any of the establishments specified in paragraph 1 shall subscribe to the Fund ; 


Provided that a break caused by 


(1) discharge in the interests of the State , or 
( 11) temporary physical unfitness which , in the opinion of the medical 

authorities , was neither due to his own neglect nor to intemperance 
or irregular habits , shall not be held to constitute a break in continu 
ity of service ." 


2 . The amendment made by this notification shall be deemed to have taken 
effect on and from the 1st April , 1960 . 

[No. F . 58 ( 2 ) -EV /60 .1 
DEVI DIYAL BHATIA , Dy . Sery.

S . O . 2123 . -- In pursuance of clause ( 3 ) of article 77 of the Constitution and of 
all other powers enabling him in this behalf, the President is pleased to make 
the following amendment in the Delegation of Financial Powers Rules , 1958 
(published as S . O . 2614 in the Gazette of India dated the 20th December, 1958 ) , 
namely : 

Amendment No. 65 
In the said Rules , the words and Administrators " may be added after the 
words " the Departments of the Central Government" occurring in column 4 
against item No. 12 ( i ) of the Annexure to Schedule V as amended by the Ministry 
of Finance , Notification No. 12 (41 ) / E . II ( A ) / 60 , dated the 22nd June 1980 ( Amend 
ment No. 55 ) . 

[No. F . 12 (41 ) - EII( A ) / 60 / 1.] 


S . O . 2124 . — In pursuance of clause ( 3 ) of article 77 of the Constitution and of 
all other powers enabling him in this behalf, the President is pleased to make 
the following amendment in the Delegation of Financial Powers Rules, 1958 
( published as S . O . 2614 in the Gazette of India dated the 20th December , 1958 ) , 
namely : 

Amendment No. 68

S . O . 2127. - - In exercise of the powers conferred by sub - section ( 2 ) of Section 
5 of the Indian Income- tax Act, 1922 ( 11 of 1922 ) and in partial modification of 
all previous notifications on the subject the Central Board of Revenue hereby 
directs that with effect from 19th August , 1960 (afternoon ) Shri H . P . Sharma, a 
Commissioner of Income- tax , shall perform all the functions of Commissioner of 
Income- tax in respect of such areas or of such persons or classes of persons or 
such incomes or classes of incomes or such cases or classes of cases as are com 
prised in the Income- tax Circles, Wards or Districts in the State of West Bengal 
as specified below : 

1. Companies District I, Calcutta , 
2 . Companies District III, Calcutta . 
3 . Midnapur. 
4 . Asansol.

Provided that he shall also perform his functions in respect of such persons 
or of such cases as have been or may be assigned by the Central Board of Revenue 
to any Income-tax Authority subordinate to him : 

Provided further that he shall not perform his functions in respect of such 
persons or such cases as have been or may be assigned to any income- tax 
authority outside his jurisdictional arca . 

While performing the said functions the said Shri Sharma shall be designated 
as the Commissioner of Income- tax with headquarters at Calcutta .

incumbent of Commissioner s post.

THE POONA CENTRAL EXCISE COLLECTORATE , POONA 

Poona, the 22nd July 1960 
S . O . 2128. - In exercise of the powers delegated to me under the Government 
of India , Ministry of Food and Agriculture Notification No . 5 - 50 /57 - Com . I/ II. 
dated 12th September , 1959 and in pursuance of Section 2 ( b ) of the Indian Cen : 
tral Oil Secds Committee Act, 1946 ( Act No. IX of 1946 ) . I hereby authorise all 
Central Excise Officers in Poona Central Excise Collectorate , not below the rank 
of Sub - Inspector to exercise within their respective jurisdiction the powers of 
" Collector " under Sections 12 and 13 of the Indian Central Oil Seeds Committee 
Act, 1946 and all Central Excise Officers in Poona Central Excise Collectorate 
not below the rank of the Superintendents to exercise within thcir respective 
jurisdiction the power under Section 12 ( 3 ) of the aforesaid Act . 

[ No. 9 / 1980 .] 


B . D . DESHMUKH , Collector. 


BOMBAY CENTRAL EXCISE COLLECTORATE 

Bombay, the 18th August 1960 
8 . 0 . 2129 . - In exercise of the powers conterred upon mę by Rule 233 of the 
Central Excise Rules, 1944 , read with Rule 51 of the said Rules , I hereby order 
that the manufacturers of Electric Motors in the Bombay Central Excise Collecto 
rate should attach to cach motor, immediately after completion of manufacture , 
a rating plate containing the following particulars : 

(i) 3 phase or Single phase. 
( ii ) Type. 
( iii ) Horse Power . 


- - - - 


- 


- - 


- 


- 


2428 THE CAVETTE OF INDIA : SITEMBER $. 194 / BHADRA 12, 1884 [PART II 

- - - - - - - - 
( iv ) R .P .M . ( Speed ). 
( v ) Volts. 
( vi) AMPs , 
( vii ) Serial No. of Electric Motor. 
( viii) Name of the manufacturer, 

2 . Where the motors are packed in deal-wood cases or other packages and 
stored in the bonded store room , the manufacturers should also indicate the above 
particulars on the packages so as to facilitate verification of H . P . rating , type, 
speed ctc . of the motors so packed , If the manufacturers have their own code 
words / letters / figures for these particulars, these should be stencilled on the 
packages instead of furnishing the particulars as above. In such cases the manu 
facturers should make available to the factory officer , the list of code words / 
letters / figures used by them for marking on the packages . 


3 . Where manufacturer s arc already affixing rating plates to the Electric 
Motor s inanufactured by them as required by the Indian Standards Institute 
stating some of the details mentioned in para 1 above, they should sec that the 
necessary additional particulars , if any , as required , are also shown on the rating 
plates allixed to the motors . 

[No. CER /233 /CX 15 /00 . 
G . KORUTHU , Collector ,

167 ( 8 ) of the Sea Customs Act , 1878. 


3 . If such an owner fails to turn up to claim the above mentioned unclaimed 
goods or to show cause against the action proposed to be taken within 30 days 
from the date of publication of this notice in the Government of India Gazette , 
the goods in question will be treated as unclaimed and the case will be decided 
accordingly .

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 3 , 1960 / BHADRA 12 , 1882 


PART II 


I have generally examined the accounts of the University for the year 1956 - 57 and on the 
basis of the general scrutiny of records and the information and explanations as furnished to me, I 
certify the accounts of the University to be correct subject to following comments :— . 
I. Hall Accounts and Accounts of various Games and Cultural Societies : 

The accounts of the various halls of the Female Education Fund and of the various Clubs 
and Societies were not produced to audit and are not certified . 


2 . Investments in various Accounts : 

The following securities are appearing on the asset side of the respective balance sheets, but 
no documents in support thereof were produced to audit. 


Particulars 

Amount 

Rs. A . P . 
(1) Sccurities reported to be in deposit with the Reserve Bank of India . 10, 000 0 0 
(ii ) Post Office Cash Certificates reported to be deposited with the 

Hydro Electric Engineer , Roorkee (Deposit Account ) . . 2 ,097 40 
( 1 ) 3 % of G . P . Note8 , 1957 (Medical College Account) . . . 1,00 ,000 O O 
(iv ) In the Balance Sheet of Special Floating Reserve Fund , an Investment of Rs. 500 in 

the Shares of Shah Jahanpur Flour Mill Co , has been shown at its par value . No 
dividend appears to have been received on those shares in the recent years and their 
market valuc could not be ascertained . 


3 . Building , 

No inventory of the land and buildings together with their individual valuation has been kept 
by the University . 
4. Medical College Fund : 


An amount of Rs. 1 ,01, 646 /2 / 11 is shown as representing the stock of Medicines. It has been 
stated that their precise value was ascertained in 1946 -47 to be Rs . 1 , 54 , 281. But no re - valuation or 
physical verification of the above stocks has been done in 1956 - 57. 


5 . Depreciation Fund Account :- - 


Lump sum amount only are provided at present for the depreciation of the various assets 
belonging to the University . A set of depreciation Rescrve Fund Rules has been framed and 
approved by the Executive Council on 30th August 1958. The rules also provide a lurap gum 
provision of Rs. one lakh on all assets. In the absence of suitable details , the adequacy or otherwise 
of the lump sum provision could not be ascertained . It is stated that depreciation Reserve Fund 
on account of the buildings held under permanent Reserve Fund and Trust Fund etc , are also ill 
cluded in the lump sum provision . 


6 , M , U . Fund Balance Sheet : 


(a ) ASSET SIDE : 


( An amount of Rs. 5, 76 , 890 / 11 / 4 has been shown as the cost of furniture , fittings , apparatus 

and equipment. An inventory of the furniture, etc . , together with the details of the 
cost working to the above figure were not available , nor docs it seem likely that it 
represents the value of the furniture , etc ., with the University as the amount has 

stood at the same figure since 1952. 
( ii ) An amount of Rs. 2 ,63 , 966 / 12 / 9 is shown under outstanding account reported to re 

present outstandings against the students on account of University fees , etc . It is 
stated that out of this sum of Rs. 82,995 / 14 / 1 has since been realised and the balance 
is irrecoverable . 


(iii ) An amount ofRs. 1 , 92 ,442 / 7 / 10 has been shown under advance on account representing 

the advances given to the various firms and to the University Officials for supplies and 
disbursements for subsequent adjustment to the final heads . No up -to -date list of 
persons or firms against whom advances arc pending was available . 


(iv ) An amount of Rs. 18 , 553 /4 /- has been shown against permanent advances to Head of the 

Departments for incurring contingent expenditure Details of persons against whom 
advances were pending were not made available .

ro . Balance Sheet of the College of Engineerlag and Technology :

Account, however , the contra entry for the above , namely “ Advances to the Engineering 
College is shown at Rs. 2 , 57 , 782 15 / 8 . The discrepancy necds reconciliation . 
11. Stocko :

materials purchased out of M . U . Floating Reserve Fund and Special Floating Reserve Fund 
respectively . In addition purchases of materials are also charged to final heads . The accounts of 
purchases from various sources are , however, not kept separately . The stock of building materials 
Chown under the Reserve Funds were , therefore , not susceptible of verification ,

NOTICE 
Bombay , the 21st July 1960 


S . O . 2143. It is hereby notified that in exercise of the powers conferred 
by clause 9 ( a ) of the Imports ( Control) Order, 1955, the Government of India , 
in the Ministry of Commerce and Industry propose to cancel the following three 
licences from the Soft Currency Area except Union of South Africa, granted 
by the Joint Chief Controller of Imports & Exports , Bombay to Messrs . Associat 
ed Industries , Bombay - 1 , unless suicient cause against this is furnished to the 
Joint Chief Controller of Imports & Exports , Bombay within ten days of the 
date of issue of this notice , by the said Messrs . Associated Industries, Bombay , 
or any Bank or any other party who may be interested in it. 

2 . In view of what is stated above, Messrs. Associated Industries, Bombay , 
are hereby directed not to enter into any commitments against the said licences 
and return these licences immediately to the Joint Chief Controller of Imports 
& Izports, Bombay .

NOTICE 

New Delhi, the 10th August 1960 
S . O . 2144 , — It is hereby notificd, that in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Clause 9 of the Imports ( Control) Order 55 , the Government of India in the 
Ministry of Commerce & Industry , propose to cancel Licence No . 0 963993 /58 /CCIY 
HQ / NQQ , dated 21st May 1959, valued at Rs, 13,450 / - for the import of Sclentific 
Instruments, Apparatus and Appliances not mainly made of rubber and also not 
made mainly of glass , as per list attached therewith , from Soft Currency Area 
except of South Africa , granted by the Chief Controller of Imports and Exports , 
New Delhi to the Principal, Chhatrapati Shivaji College, the Rayat Shikshan 
Sanstha, Satara Camp, Satara , unless sufficient cause against this is furnished 
to the Chief Controller of Imports & Exports , New Delhi, within ten days of 
Issue of this notice , by the said Principal, Chhatrapati Shivaji College , the Rayat

MINISTRY OF FOOD AND AGRICULTURE 

( Department of Agriculture ) 

New Delhi, the 26th August 1960 

JUTE GRADING AND MARKING RULES , 1960 
S . O . 2155 . — 1. Short title and application . — These Rules may be called the Jute 
Grading and Marking Rules, 1960 . 

( 2 ) They shall apply to raw jute of the trade descriptions grown in India and 
specified in the Schedules to these rules, 

2. Grade designations. - Grade designations to indicate the characteristics and 
quality of jute of specified tradę descriptions are set out in column 1 of Schedules 
II and III to these rules. 


3 . Definition of quality - The definition of quality indicated by the grade 
designations is specified in columns 2 to 5 of Schedules II and III to these rules . 


4 . Grade designation mark . — The grade designation mark to be applied to 
each bale shall consist of a label bearing the design set out in Schedule I to these 
rules specifying the grade designation . 


5 . Method of marking . The grade designation mark label shall be securely 
attached to each bale in a manner approved by the Agricultural Marketing 
Adviser to the Government of India In addition to the grade designation mark , 
the following particulars shall be clearly marked on the label: 


( a ) Serial number. 
(b ) Trade description . 
( c ) Year of harvest. 
( d ) Date of pressing . 
( e ) Place of packing and district 


6 . Metliod of packing . – Jute shall be packed in bales of customary weight in 
a manner approved by the Agricultural Marketing Adviser to the Government of 
India .

and Disposals (Machine Supervisor ) Recruitment Rules, 1960 . 

2 . Application . These rules shall apply for recruitment to the post specified 
in column 1 of the Schedule annexed hercto . 

3 . Number , classifloation and scale of pay . - The number , classification of the

recruitment, the age limit , qualifications and other matters connected therewith

relaxed in the case of Schedule Castes / Scheduled Tribes and Displaced persons 
and other special categories in accordance with the general orders issued from 
time to time by the Government of India .

and no female candidate who has married a person having already a wife living 
shall be eligible for appointment to the post : 

Provided that the Government of India may , if it is satisfied that there cxist 
special grounds for doing so , exempt any such candidate from the operation of 
this rule .

MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT 

New Delhi, the 24th August 1960 
S . O . 2166 . — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
( 14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award 
of the Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad , in the industrial dispute between the 
employers in relation to the Sirka Colliery and their workmen . 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL , 

DHANBAD 

REFERENCE No . 29 OF 1960 
PARTILS : 
Employers in relation to the Sirka Colliery 

AND 
Their workmen . 

PRESENT: 
Shri G . Palit, M . A ., B .L ., Chairman , 

Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad . 
APPEARANCES: 

Shri S . S . Mukherjea, Advocate — for the employers. 

Shri S . K . Mukherjee , Advocate — for the workmen . 
STATE : Bihar, 

INDUSTRY; Coal. 
Dhanbad , dated the 16th August , 1960 

AWARD 
The Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India , by its Order 
No. 2 / 76 /60 -LRII, dated the 25th May 1960 , made in pursuance of the powers 
conferred by clause ( d ) of sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (XIV of 1947 ) , referred the aforesaid industrial dispute to 
the Central Government Industrial Tribunal at Dhanbad presided over by me 
for adjudication of the matters contained as per schedule below : 
" Having regard to the duties performed by Shri Abisal Khalko , Clerk , 

Sirka Colliery , whether the management of that colliery is justified 
in keeping him in Grade III as per provisions of the Award of the 
All India Industrial Tribunal ( Colliery Disputes ) as modificd by 
the Labour Appellate Tribunal ? If not, to what relief is he entitled 
after the 7th December 1959 ? ” 


AM 


2 . Turning to the contention of the workman , I get that he wants to be 
upgraded and placed on clerical grade II with retrospective effect on the ground 
of his present duties . His further contention is that he was originally an 
Attendance Clerk . When Attendance Clerks were re - designated as Register 


2572 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: SEPTEMBER 9, 1960 / BHADRA 19 , 1882 


PART IL 


Keepers , he was placed in Grade III after the publication of the Majumdar 
Award . As a matter of fact, he was doing the work of Assistant Cashier or 
the Cash Cle . k . He also enumerates the duties performed by him in his written 
statement, namely , that he was making payments to the workmen , he was 
posting cash ledgers , making out outward bills , maintaining bill register and 
unclaimed wages register, filling up the cost per ton sheet, etc . But in his 
oral evidence before me Shri Abisai Khalko has admitted that when the cashier 
is on leave, the Assistant Cashier takes over the charge of cash , He never 
took charge of the cash from 1950 up till now . During the leave vacancy of 
the cashier all his duties were performed by the Assistant Cashier . It appears 
that he does not handle cash except that he gives the sick khoraki and bonus 
to persons on sick leave. So the claim in his statement that he is an Assistant 
Cashier admittedly falls through . Regarding the other duties, I also get from 
his admission that the cost of the revenue figures are prepared by the Cashier 
and he prepares copies thereof. It is the Cashier who prepares the cost per 
ton sheet and Shri Khalko merely prepares copies . The Assistant Cashier also 
maintains Exhibits A and A ( 1 ) . So practically he does not prepare anything 
besides routine duties of a clerk of the minimum grade. He is not required 
to discharge any onerous responsibility . Even if he was a Register Keeper, 
as all Attendance Clerks were , then in the light of the foot-note to Appendix 
XVI of the All India Industrial Tribunal (Colliery Disputes) he cannot occupy 
a better grade than Grade III. So considering his status as well as his nature 
of duties performed by him I think the company was justifled in placing him 
in Grade III as it did . 

3 . Having regard to the above, I find that he is entitled to no relief after 
7th December 1959 as raised in the issue having been rightly put into Grede 
III. So the case of the workman falls through . I make no order for costs 

DHANBAD ; 
The 16th August, 1960 . 

(Sd.) G . PALIT, Chairman , 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal, 

Dhanbad. 

[No. 2 / 76 / 60 -LRII. ] 
New Delhi, the 27th August 1960 
s . O , 2167, — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
( 14 of 1947) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad, in the industrial dispute between the emplo 
yers in relation to the Pure Golukdih Colliery and their workmen . 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL , 

DHANBAD 

REFERENCE No. 22 OF 1960. 
PARTIES ; 
Employers in relation to the Pure Golukdih colliery , Post Office Jharla , 

District Dhanbad , 


AND 


Their workmen . 
PRESENT : Shri G . Palit, M .A . B.L ., Chairman , 

Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad. 
APPEARANCES : 

Shri J. N . Mukherjee, Manager - for, the employers. . 
Shri B . N . Sharma, Member Executive Committee , Colliery Mazdoor 

Sangh , for the workmen . 
STATE : Bihar . 

INDUSTRY : Coal. 
Dhanbad , dated the 18th August 1960 

AWARD . 
The Ministry of Labour and Employment, Government of India , by its Order 
No . 2 / 27 / 60 - LR II dated the 10th May 1960, made in pursuance of the powers 


SEC, $ ( ii ) 


THE GAZET IE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 9, 1960 / BHADRA 12 , 1882 2572 


conferred by clause ( d ) of sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 10 of the Industrial Dis 
putes Act , 1947 (XIV of 47) , referred the aforesaid dispute for adjudication to 
the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad , presided over by me , 
concerning the matters as per schedule below : 


SCHEDULE 


" Whether the management of Pure Colukdih colliery , Pure Golukdih , Coal 

Company, Post Office Jharia ( Dhanbad ) was justified in dismissing 
Shri Nagwantlal, Trammer ? If not, to what relief is the workman 
entitled ? 


2 , Shri Nagwantlal has been hauled up by the management under two sepa 
rate charges viz . ( 1 ) that he intentionally violated the specific instruction of 
his superior by hauling up five loaded coal tubs in place of three. ( 2 ) That 
though his leave application for six days from 6th July , 1959 to 11th July , 1959 
was disallowed , he availed himself of the said leave from 6th July , 1959. He 
further continued in his absence till 23rd July , 1959 . So he was absent without 
permission for more than 10 days. 


3 . The workman , on the other hand , takes up the plea that the trammers all 
along hauled up four or five tubs and that the breakage of the bracket of the 
haulage rope had taken place in normal course of things because it was old . 
Regarding the second charge his contention is that he turned up on the 18th 
July , 1959 but was not permitted to join . So his absence did not last for moro 
than 10 days . Further, he contends that he is the Assistant Secretary of the 
Union and was a protected person under the Act. Because of his union activi 
ties he has been victimised . 


4 . Turning to the evidence, I find that there were regular charge shects issued 
to the workman . Domestic enquiries were held in which the said workman did 
participate . He was found guilly of both the charges . As a consequence he was 
dismissed from service. Now the point is whether this dismissal is justified. 


If I turn to the standing orders of coal mining industry, I And Nagwantlal 
has been charged under para 27 ( 1 ) of the said standing orders so far as his 
intentional insubordination of the order of the superior is concerned . That the 
order in question not to haul up more than 3 tubs does not appear to be un 
reasonable . This order being of the Manager and the Electrical Supervisor 
amounts to an order of a superior oficer , Now the point is whether there was 
wilful insubordination or disobedience of such order , For this two things are 
necessary , viz . ( 1 ) that the order was duly communicated and ( 2 ) that the 
workman did deliberately violate this order . It is in evidence that the order 
in question is not in writing . If it is there, it is only on the Board . All that 
is said is that the orders were verbally communicated . Mere announcement on 
the Board is not enough particularly when illiterate people are concerned . I 
find no reason why he there should be nothing in writing of such an important 
order which ensured the safety of the haulage rope . No motive in the present 
case has been alleged or proved why these trammers would take it into their 
heads to violate this order if it was enforced . It is the definite contention of the 
workman in his reply to the charge sheet that all trammers have all along been 
hauling up five tubs at a time and that there was no specific order of the mana 
ger to the contrary , Thus the order if it was there was observed more in breach 
than in observance . So it was the incumbent duty of the management in face 
of this emphatic denial to prove that, as a matter of fact more than three tube 
were never hauled up . Of course , some evidence has been adduced in the 
domestic enquiry . But I find that there was no cross -examination of the wit 
nesses by the workman or anybody on his behalf . This testimony , not being 
tested by cross - examination and taken by an officer of the management by itself 
is not sufficient to prove the guilt . I really fail to find any reason why the 
management would fight shy and not examine any of those witnesses though 
they are in its employ and are available for making deposition in court. So 
having regard to all these facts and circumstances , I am unable to agree with 
the finding of the management, namely , that there was wilful insubordination 
or disobedience of the order of a superior although such order was lawful and 
reasonable . The plea that the Haulage rope was old has taled . So I am 


2574 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 9 , 1960 / BHADRA 12, 1882 


PART II 


however unable to find that the management was 
workman on this count, 


justified in dismissing this 


6 . Turning to the second charge, I And from the admission of Nagwantlal, the 
workman in question that his leave was not allowed . His application for leave 
was dated 29th June, 1959. According to him a letter was served on him at his 
village residence on 30th June, 1959 that the leave was not allowed . He admits 
to have countersigned the copy of the said letter . He further admits that though 
his leave was not allowed , he left the colliery on 6th July , 1959 . His defence 
is that on 10th July , 1959 he reported for duty but the Manager did not per 
mit him to join . So his contention is that he is within the protecting clause 16 
of para 27 of the standing orders. But I am unable to accept his defence on this 
point. Firstly , there is the evidence of the management that though he turned 
upon 16th July , 1959, he did not want to join . It was on 23rd July , that he 
actually offered to join . Even if I accept the defence of the workman on this 
point, namely , that he was within the safeguard of clause 16 of para 27 of the 
standing orders, I find that his plea cannot stand him in good stead . Firstly I 
refer to para 12 of the said standing orders. There it is stated that any employee 
who remains absent without properly approved leave will render himself liable 
for disciplinary action . That he was absent without approved leave is beyond 
question . He could not show any satisfactory cause of his remaining absent 
which is required even under clause 16 of para 27 of the standing orders . Au that 
is said is that he had urgent work at home. That can never be enough in such 
circumstances . Besides , I think that he is guilty under clause ( 1 ) of para 27 
of the aforesaid standing orders . When his leave was refused his remaining 
away from work or availing of the leave amounts to wilful insubordination . The 
order refusing to sanction leave does not appear to be either unlawful or un 
reasonable . The reason given is that no leave was due and that no substitute 
arrangement could be arranged . None of these grounds have been challenged , 
before me. His availing of the leave in spite of its refusal amounts to inten 
tional violation of the reasonable order of a superior. So he was rightly found 
guilty by the management and I agree with the said Anding. 


7 . Now , regarding the allegation of victimisation , I am unable to find , that 
the management was motivated to take this action against the workman merely 
for his union activities. If that was so , then the grounds on which he had been 
let down would have been either false or flimsy . But in this case , though I 
could not agree with the finding that the workman was guilty under the first 
charge of hauling up more than three tubs at a time, but I agree with the finding 
of the management that the workman did defy the management. Such action 
is certainly subversive of discipline . I can never countenance such action in 
the interest of the industry . Protected person does not carry any immunity to 
a workman to do anything he pleases and to sabotage discipline . Protected 
person under the new Act only places such a person on a preferential footing 
with regard to the obtaining of permission for taking action under Section 33 of 
the Act by providing that express permission in writing of the Tribunal concern 
ed should be taken as a condition precedent. But nothing more. But in this 
case this workman seems to have thought that because he happens to be the 
Assistant Secretary of the Union he could disregard the standing orders of the 
company and defy the management s order even though the said order is reason 
able and lawful. 

8 . So having regard to all these facts and also circumstances, elicited before 
me, I find that the company was justified in dismissing Shri Nagwantlal, Tram 
mer . As such , he is not entitled to any relief from this Tribunal. . 

Sd./ - G . PALIT , Chairman , 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal, 

Dhanbad. 
Dhanbad, 
The 18th August , 1960 ,

$ . O . 2169 . — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
( 14 of 1947 ) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
he National Industrial Tribunal ( Bank Disputes ), Bombay in the matter of an 
application under section 33A of the said Act from Shri S . Natarajan of the 
Devkaran Nanjee Banking Company Limited , Bombay . 


BEFORE THE NATIONAL INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (BANK DISPUTES ) 

AT BOMBAY 
COMPLAINT No . 8 OF 1960 

IN REFERENCE. No . 1 OF 1960 
Shri S . Natarajan , Mathews Building, 1st Floor , Bhandarwada Road , Shivaji 
Park , Dadar, Bombay - 28 _ Complainant. 

Versus 
M / s. Devkaran Nanjee Banking Co . Ltd ., Horniman Circle , Fort, Bombay - 1 
Respondent. 
PRESENT 
· Honourable Shri Justice K . T. Desai, Presiding Oncer, National Industrial 
Tribunal (Bank Disputes ), Bombay - 1 , 

APPEARANCES 
Shri G . R . Venkataraman - for the Respondent - Bank . 
Shri S . Natarajan ---Complainant in person . 

INDUSTRY : Banking . 
Bombay, dated the 27th July , 1960 

AWARD 
This complaint purports to be one under Section 33A of the Industrial Dis 
putes Act, 1947, by Shri S . Natarajan , an employce of M / s . Devkaran Nanjee 
Banking Co , Ltd ., which is the respondent in this case . The Complainant states 
that the management of the Bank indulged in a provocative act during the pen 
dency of the proceedings before this National Tribunal by changing his service 
condition to his prejudice. In amplification of his complaint , he states that he 
had been deprived of his usual annual increment for the year 1960 apart from 
the denial of two increments for upgradation to Class I with effect from January, 
1960 . He further states that at a time when his representation in respect of 
Special Supervisory Allowance payable as per directions in para No . 164 ( B ) 9 of 


Src , 3 ( ii) 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 8 , 1960 / BIIADRA 12 , 1RA2 2577 


the " Shastri Award " is pending before the Ministry of Labour and Employment, 
the Management, in order to defeat his casc for Supervisory Allowance , changed 
his designation to that of an Officer . 


Usual Notices were issued to both the parties fixing the date of hearing on 
27th July 1960 . 


The Respondent- Bank by its written statement dated the 21st July 1960 has 
denied all the charges made by the Complainant and stated that it has not com 
mitted any provocative act , much less it has committed any act which could be 
construed as contravening Section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 . It 
further states that the Complainant, who is an officer of the Bank and who is 
not governed by the terms of any Award applicable to the Bank , was not entitled 
to any increments as a matter of right or otherwise , and that the story of change 
in designation or the effect, if any , of the use of the word Officer as alleged was 
also false . It also denied the knowledge of any alleged claim pending before the 
Ministry of Labour and Employment. The Respondent- Bank contends that by 
merely calling him an officer there has been no change in the lawful rank of the 
complainant nor was there any change in the terms of conditions of his service 
or in any of his rights whatever they be under the law . 


This matter came up for hearing to -day when Shri Natarajan appeared in 
person in support of his case and Shri Venkataraman of the Indian Banks Asso 
ciation represented the Respondent- Bank . " The arguments of the Complainant 
were heard . 


The alleged non - implementation of the Shastri Award in the case of the com 
plainant is of a long- standing and does not constitute an alteration in the service 
conditions of the Complainant during the pendency of the proceedings before this 
National Tribunal. It is the contention of the Respondent that the provisions 
of the Shastri Award are not applicable to the Complainant. As regards the 
designation of the Complainant as an Officer , apart from all other considera 
tions, Shri G . R . Venkataraman , on behalf of the Bank , stated that the Complain 
ant in this case was a workman within the mcaning of the amended definition of 
workman under the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, and that the Bank will pro 
ceed with the present reference before this National Tribunal on that footing . He 
further stated that by calling the Complainant an Omcer it was never the inten 
tion of the Bank to make him cease to be a workman within the meaning of the 
amended definition of workman . 


I am satisfied that the designation of the Complainant as officer does not 
constitute an alteration in the service conditions of the complainant within the 
meaning of Section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 . Shri Natarajan was 
designated as officer in the past and the same practice continued even after the 
constitution of this National Tribunal. 


The Complainant has made out no case under Section 33A of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 and his complaint is dismissed . No order as to costs . 


KANTILAL T . DESAI, 

Presiding omcer , 
National Industrial Tribunal ( Bank Disputes ), Bombay - 1. 

[No. LRII - 10 ( 125 ) /00 .1 


New Delhi, the 29th August 1960

AWARD 
These four applications, purporting to be under Section 33A of the Industrial 
Disputes Act , were fled by the complainants above named , who are members of 
the editorial stall of the Respondents , the Press Trust of India , in respect of certain 
prejudicial changes alleged to have been effected during the pendency of this 
dispute by the Respondents in the duties allocated to them . The applications 
involve a common question and were heard together and are, therefore , being 
disposed of by a single award . 

After the usual notices were issued and the Respondents had filed their written 
statements , the complaints were taken up for hearing and at the adjourned hear . 
ing on 12th August 1960 , the parties filed consent terms on which the applicants 
had agreed to withdraw these applications. These four applications are , therefore , 


SEC , 3 (11)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : SEPTEMBER 3, 1960 / BHADRA 12, 1882 2579 


dismissed as withdrawn . A copy of the joint application of the parties comprising 
the consent terms ig attached herewith and marked Annexure A , and the same 
shall form part of this award . 
No order as to costs .

AWARD 
During the pendency of Reference No . 2 (NTB ) of 1959, in which the appli 
cant is a workman concerned, the present application was Aled purporting to be 
under section 33A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , alleging that the respon 
dents, the Press Trust of India , had made certain changes in respect of the paid 
holidays to which the applicant was entitled on the date this dispute was referred 
to this Tribunal. 

After the complaint was heard for some time the applicant at the hearing on 
12th August 1960 Aled an application stating that he desired to withdraw the 
complaint, to which the management does not object. The complaint will there 
fore , stand dismissed as withdrawn , A copy of the application for withdrawal of 
the complaint annexed hereto will form part of this award . 
No order as to costs ,
